- Cycles 27, 51, 75, and 166 were excluded as distinct prior goal-15 cells. The remaining Taproot PSBT parser cell was confirmed: BIP371 public-key metadata accepted invalid all-zero x-only values in input script-signature, input BIP32 derivation, input internal-key, output internal-key, and output BIP32 derivation fields. BIP340 `lift_x` and the existing `XOnlyPubKey::IsFullyValid()` define the required curve-validity contract; arbitrary v1 witness programs are a separate intentional BIP341 domain.
- Source/test/journal commit `6b64106217` (`psbt: validate Taproot x-only pubkeys`) adds the five validation checks and malformed-field regressions, updates two existing value-boundary fixtures to use valid `NUMS_H` bytes, and is authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`. The pre-fix acceptance control passed 8 assertions; the post-fix focused test passed 5 assertions. Full normal `psbt_tests` passed 14 cases and 149 assertions before and after the commit.
- Independent Clang 19 UBSan/alignment/object-size rebuild and PSBT suite passed 14 cases and 149 assertions. The post-commit normal rebuild exited 0 and the post-commit `psbt_tests` rerun passed 14/149. Exact logs and BIP371/BIP340 links are in `agent-journal/public-object-validation.md` and `/data/my_storage/tmp/cycle168-*`.
- The complete 1,229-case normal unit suite could not provide a code result because the root filesystem hit the test disk guard and a block-file flush I/O failure. Two cycle-168 full-suite attempts were stopped after the overlap was discovered; only those owned processes were stopped. Generated test scratch was cleaned where no active descriptors existed. Persistent PIDs `777094` and `956381` were preserved. No owned test, fuzz, sanitizer, daemon, or profiling process remains.
- Verdict: confirmed and fixed. The remaining goal-15 queue is direct address/key decoding, malformed public API wrappers, and release-version parser differences. Do not reopen the Taproot x-only metadata cell without a distinct field, caller, backend, or recurrence signal. A separate state-only close commit follows; then the next cycle must run a fresh gate and exact selector.

## Cycle 167 Completion

- The fresh gate after Cycle 166 started at `c95f1fd976f0fe4914ac2ad8393269cdc8f2ab4c`, with `origin/master` `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and divergence `42 1115`. The exact selector returned `32` (`history-incomplete-fixes`), and the dedicated branch is `uber-cycle-167-history-incomplete-fixes-20260730`. Catalog, protocol, and corrected-goal TSV hashes stayed `5c847ef77405df14b7e7e8fa50430d11a71dcbac3d84df66d25a168d1e955ea8`, `954a67b016918eb2d71c17ae78a12b38f014bb47ed32fe45a0b6f307e5002fc0`, and `babfb36e1a64d8b4ad310459306fa2dfdb240d644d731e2b795177f93a68f1cb`.
- Cycles 43, 58, and 137 were excluded. The distinct remaining migration-side cell was startup settings persistence outside `ApplyMigrationData()`. Historical `0bf7b38bff` introduced auxiliary-wallet settings writes before the migration transaction, and `4acd063ba6` changed their load flag without moving that boundary. Locally created auxiliary wallets use an empty context, so failure cleanup resets them without `RemoveWallet()` and can leave a deleted wallet in `settings.json`.
- The existing `test_failed_migration_cleanup` reproduced this on a BDB-to-SQLite migration: before the fix, the focused run passed its old cleanup assertions but left `node0/regtest/settings.json` with `wallet: [default_wallet, failed_watchonly]` after the `failed_watchonly` directory was removed. The pre-existing `failed_solvables` database remained untouched. The source/test/journal commit `7832fdb47357d9646100f7582bab5c219512fd3d` (`wallet: avoid stale auxiliary settings after failed migration`) defers both auxiliary settings writes until `RunWithinTxn()` commits and asserts both failed names are absent from persisted settings. It is authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- The wallet-enabled v28.4 release build completed with BDB and SQLite support. The current wallet-enabled `bitcoind` rebuilt successfully with `CCACHE_DIR=/data/my_storage/tmp/cycle167-current-ccache`. The post-fix focused functional run, repeated after commit, passed; settings contained only `default_wallet`. The temporary pre-fix ordering mutation rebuilt and failed at the new `failed_watchonly` assertion, independently proving the oracle. `git diff --check` and the final build passed. No owned daemon/test process remains; unrelated PIDs `777094` and `956381` and all known untracked artifacts were preserved.
- Verdict: confirmed local migration/restart correctness defect and fixed. Detailed evidence and the next migration queue are in `agent-journal/history-incomplete-fixes.md`. A separate state-only close commit follows; the next cycle must perform a fresh gate, run the exact selector, and avoid reopening this settings-transaction cell unless a distinct migration mode or recurrence is found.

## Cycle 166 Completion

- The fresh gate after Cycle 165 was clean at `2691ebfa27984989a1f19ed390ce79e4cfd4ba41`, with `origin/master` `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and divergence `1113 42`. The catalog, protocol, and corrected goals TSV hashes remained `5c847ef77405df14b7e7e8fa50430d11a71dcbac3d84df66d25a168d1e955ea8`, `954a67b016918eb2d71c17ae78a12b38f014bb47ed32fe45a0b6f307e5002fc0`, and `babfb36e1a64d8b4ad310459306fa2dfdb240d644d731e2b795177f93a68f1cb`.
- The exact selector returned `15` (`public-object-validation`), which remained eligible for a new direct-parser/API-wrapper cell after the prior P2PK and x-only descriptor inference cells were excluded. The dedicated branch is `uber-cycle-166-public-object-validation-20260730`.
- `CPubKey::RecoverCompact()` masked its compact-signature header before validating it. Since `CKey::SignCompact()` defines headers `27..34`, a deterministic 0..255 probe found noncanonical headers such as `0`, `3`, `4`, `7`, `8`, and `11` recovering the same valid keys as canonical headers. The fix rejects the out-of-domain header before deriving the recovery ID or compression bit, and the direct `RecoverCompact()` plus `MessageVerify()` regressions are in source commit `c5de16acf3` (`pubkey: reject noncanonical compact headers`), authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- Independent evidence: the post-fix probe accepted only `27`, `28`, `31`, and `32` for the selected signature. The corrected Clang 19 TSAN build passed `key_tests` with 15 cases/897 assertions and `util_tests` with 80 cases/3,994 assertions; one-case reruns of both new regressions passed. Removing only the range check made both regressions fail (key recovery returned a valid key; the message wrapper did not return `ERR_PUBKEY_NOT_RECOVERED`). Exact logs and the invalid parallel harness attempt are recorded in `agent-journal/public-object-validation.md` and `/data/my_storage/tmp/cycle166-*`.
- The first broad test attempt used a nonexistent `TMPDIR` and concurrent stateful binaries; it was classified as harness setup failure, the owned processes were interrupted, and all evidence above was rerun serially with an isolated temp directory. No owned test, fuzz, sanitizer, daemon, or profiling process remains. Persistent unrelated PIDs `777094` and `956381` and all unrelated untracked artifacts remain preserved.
- Verdict: confirmed local public-parser/cross-wrapper contract defect and fixed. The detailed journal is `agent-journal/public-object-validation.md`. Remaining goal-15 queue: direct address/key decoding, malformed API-wrapper inputs, and release-version parser differences. The next cycle must perform a fresh gate, reroll only if the selected cell is explicitly closed, and run the exact selector command.

## Cycle 164 Completion

- The post-Cycle-163 gate was clean at `98f0c7b4eac40756eb6c2441818b2d013516e324`,
  with origin/master `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base
  `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and divergence `42 1109`.
  The exact selector returned `35`, which was rerolled because Cycle 107
  already closed mutation testing; the required reroll returned `30`
  (`security-logging`). The dedicated branch is
  `uber-cycle-164-security-logging-20260730`.
- The audit found that `ArgsManager::LogArgs()` applied `SENSITIVE` redaction
  to config and command-line values but emitted `rw_settings` values through
  `setting.second.write()` unchanged. `settings.json` is read before startup
  logging, and registered values such as `rpcpassword` are marked SENSITIVE,
  so a persistent password could reach `debug.log` in plaintext. The pre-fix
  `getarg_tests/logargs` regression exited 201 with the captured `private42`
  and no masked setting line.
- `LogArgs()` now resolves each settings key with `InterpretKey`, obtains the
  same argument flags, and emits `****` for sensitive settings. The existing
  logargs test covers the persistent map. Source/test/journal commit
  `6da617fd728ba15188f8ef89feb3b5673ae02ddf` (`args: redact sensitive
  settings in startup logs`) is authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- After the fix, `getarg_tests/logargs` seed `164002` passed all 7 assertions;
  `getarg_tests,argsman_tests` seed `164003` passed 25 cases and 220787
  assertions. A scratch `regtest/settings.json` containing
  `{"rpcpassword":"cycle164-secret"}` was exercised through the UBSan
  `bitcoind` startup path. The output contained `Setting file arg: rpcpassword = ****`
  and never the secret; the bounded daemon run reached `Shutdown done` with
  expected timeout status 124.
- The detailed ledger is `agent-journal/security-logging.md`. Remaining
  security-logging queue: attacker-controlled peer/message/error strings for
  truthfulness, escaping, and bounded repetition, followed by diagnostic
  severity and rate-limit review. The next cycle must perform a fresh gate
  and exact selector draw. PIDs `777094` and `956381` and unrelated untracked
  artifacts remain preserved.

## Cycle 163 Completion

- The fresh gate after Cycle 162 kept the catalog, prompt, and goals TSV hashes
  at `5c847ef77405df14b7e7e8fa50430d11a71dcbac3d84df66d25a168d1e955ea8`,
  `10408ad01c000bba65c1fff135cf2d7d92508bf8a8549141e3d6880f7fe0d4ec`, and
  `babfb36e1a64d8b4ad310459306fa2dfdb240d644d731e2b795177f93a68f1cb`.
  The exact selector sequence was `58`, `62`, `7`, `8`; the first three were
  closed cells and were rerolled. Goal 8 (`locking-threading`) was accepted on
  branch `uber-cycle-163-locking-threading-20260730` at start HEAD
  `c7aeed287d4426b0af928137ba41e14cc375b754`, with origin/master
  `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base
  `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and start divergence `42 1107`.
- The distinct open cell was the mapport lifecycle shared by GUI option
  changes and core shutdown. `StartThreadMapPort()` assigned the global
  `std::thread` while `StopMapPort()`/`InterruptMapPort()` read it without
  synchronization. A two-thread barrier test reproduced a TSan race in the
  original implementation: seed `163007` exited 66 with a write in
  `std::thread::operator=` racing a `joinable()` read, identified as global
  `g_mapport_thread`.
- `src/mapport.cpp` now protects all lifecycle entry points with a file-scope
  `GlobalMutex`, keeps interrupt plus join atomic for disable, and interrupts
  inside `StopMapPortInternal()` before joining. The permanent
  `pcp_tests/mapport_lifecycle_is_serialized` test uses a null socket factory,
  a barrier, and 32 concurrent enable/disable iterations. Source/test/journal
  commit `45129ce6b6027effb78d6301f2f093b9378328d1` is
  `mapport: serialize lifecycle operations`, authored as
  `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- The fixed Clang 19 ThreadSanitizer binary (`a77f6a5301b29771508858913c36a3dd3e9980f6500959322298da1b4fea34f`)
  passed the lifecycle test with seed `163014`, one case and one assertion,
  with no report. The fixed Clang 19 UBSan/alignment/object-size binary
  (`fd7d77de6838316e9dafc03b2a43be6b3c977917e5c0d2d0d79dabe53771ed6b`) passed
  `pcp_tests` seed `163015` (13 cases/213 assertions), `net_tests` seed
  `163016` (36/146816), `denialofservice_tests` seed `163017` (5/90), and
  `scheduler_tests` seed `163018` (4/27). `git diff --check` passed.
- TSan configuration initially hit the installed Cap'n Proto 0.9.2/Clang 19
  C++20 incompatibility and a broken ccache symlink; the successful isolated
  build used `-DENABLE_IPC=OFF -DWITH_CCACHE=OFF`. The persistent unrelated
  processes PIDs `777094` and `956381` and all unrelated untracked artifacts
  were preserved. The detailed evidence is in
  `agent-journal/locking-threading.md`.
- Verdict: confirmed and fixed. The next mapport queue cell is platform-specific
  worker network behavior during shutdown; do not reopen the fixed thread
  lifecycle race unless its ownership or call-thread contract changes. A
  separate state-only close commit follows, then the next cycle must run a
  fresh gate and exact selector draw.

## Cycle 162 Completion

- Exact selector: the fresh gate drew `86` (`bitcoin-chainstate-symmetry`). The dedicated branch is `uber-cycle-162-bitcoin-chainstate-symmetry-20260730`; start HEAD was `7bd126c42ffd0cea05e87efa12d6ea48e237743b`, `origin/master` was `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base was `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and start divergence was `1105 42`.
- Prior Cycle 142 block-file flush-publication work and Cycle 135 BlockFilterIndex cursor work were explicitly excluded. The distinct remaining cell was directory durability after file data commit. `FlatFileSeq::Flush` ignored `DirectoryCommit`, whose `void` implementation discarded directory-open and `fsync` failures. The 2018 directory-sync rationale and LevelDB's analogous `EINVAL`-only tolerance established the intended contract.
- A Linux preload harness injected one `EIO` into the blocks-directory `fsync` after block-file `fdatasync`. The pre-fix `/data/my_storage/tmp/cycle89-build/bin/test_bitcoin` (SHA-256 `fc05b6e538769184b263de31f6eae991aecc2bdc70b3de9b997773df0acb3edc`) ran the existing `chainstate_write_tests/flush_failure_stops_metadata_publication` oracle, logged `ChainStateFlushed`, and failed its `!flushed`, `state.IsError()`, and `!sub->m_did_flush` assertions. This independently confirmed metadata publication after a failed durability step.
- `DirectoryCommit` now returns `bool`, reports open/sync/close errors, tolerates only `EINVAL`, and is `[[nodiscard]]`; `FlatFileSeq::Flush` propagates a directory failure as a block-file flush failure. The source/test/journal commit is `50c503d0dcb7086d940fb2638ea19251c561adfc` (`flatfile: propagate directory commit failures`), authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`. The test adds direct missing-directory coverage.
- The patched Clang 19 UBSan build (`5e47cde669521b2ea9d0943cf282dd2e717ff609226feb3e237e297b89875344`) passed the same preload oracle with no `ChainStateFlushed` and `*** No errors detected`. Clean focused suites passed: `fs_tests`, `flatfile_tests`, `chainstate_write_tests`, `blockmanager_tests`, `blockfilter_index_tests`, `validation_flush_tests`, and `validation_chainstatemanager_tests`. `git diff --check` passed.
- Limitations: fault injection was Linux/ELF-specific; no actual power-loss or dm-flakey replay was attempted, and the Windows no-op directory-sync path remains platform-specific. The external harness is `agent-journal/cycle162_directory_fsync_fail.c`; it remains an untracked probe along with all pre-existing unrelated artifacts. PIDs `777094` and `956381` were preserved and untouched.
- Verdict: confirmed local durability/publication defect and fixed. The detailed journal is `agent-journal/bitcoin-chainstate-symmetry.md`. The next queue is prune unlink-directory durability after successful flat-file flush, other atomic rename users, and index publication/restart behavior under database or directory-sync faults. A separate state-only close commit follows; the next cycle must perform a fresh gate and exact selector draw.

## Cycle 161 Completion

- Exact selector sequence: the fresh gate first drew `84` (`secp-nonce-session`), which was already closed by Cycle 95; the required exact reroll `shuf -i 0-98 -n 1` drew `20` (`micro-optimization`). The dedicated branch is `uber-cycle-161-micro-optimization-20260730`; start HEAD was `41fb712ad28f701dec477ea9d5245c1ff1a4e4f3`, `origin/master` was `67efced1fc83a0b7215cc1513e7c4754fee0f12f`, merge-base was `a2aab6df97d9f3e1186e8c3fc57ad909cc8aef9b`, and start divergence was `42 1103`.
- Cycle 140's `BufferedFile::FindByte`/`memchr` cell was excluded. This cycle measured a distinct `HexStr` loop in `src/crypto/hex_base.cpp`. The tested candidate replaced the existing two-byte `memcpy` from the lookup table with direct `it[0]`/`it[1]` stores.
- The same GCC 12.2 `RelWithDebInfo` (`-O2 -g`, `REDUCE_EXPORTS=ON`) build produced five CPU-2-pinned base/candidate pairs in each execution order. Base-first averages were `0.002207324` seconds, `24,000,403.926` instructions, and `1,774,230.198` cycles for base versus `0.003073632` seconds, `36,000,404.723` instructions, and `2,467,150.864` cycles for candidate. Candidate-first averages were `0.002196625` seconds, `24,000,403.918` instructions, and `1,766,416.644` cycles versus `0.003095499` seconds, `36,000,405.163` instructions, and `2,484,963.980` cycles. Per 4,000,000-byte batch, the candidate regressed from about `6.00` to `9.00` instructions/byte and `0.44` to `0.62` cycles/byte in both orders.
- The candidate binary passed `util_tests/util_HexStr` (one case, no errors), which covers empty input, all supported span types, and every byte value. A first broad test invocation used a nonexistent `TMPDIR` and entered an unrelated long-running path; that newly started process was stopped after recording the setup failure. Persistent PIDs `777094` and `956381` were preserved and untouched. No source/test change is justified; the source was restored exactly and `git diff --check` passed.
- Verdict: dismissed as a performance regression. Detailed evidence, exact commands, binary hashes, and limitations are in `agent-journal/micro-optimization.md`. Journal-only close commit `3cee083ae0` (`uber-goal: close cycle 161 micro optimization`) is authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`. A separate state-only close commit follows; the next cycle must perform a fresh gate and exact selector draw.

- Added explicit destructors that cleanse both complete SHA members and placement-storage regressions for both HMAC types. The repository has no HMAC copy/assignment call sites; copyability was left unchanged to avoid a public API break. Existing digest output is covered by the HMAC vectors.
- `CCACHE_DIR=/data/my_storage/tmp/cycle152-ccache cmake --build /data/my_storage/tmp/cycle89-build --target test_bitcoin -j2` passed after an initial environment-only ccache-directory failure. Optimized Clang destructor disassembly retained two `memory_cleanse` calls per context with `0x68`-byte `CSHA256` and `0xc8`-byte `CSHA512` lengths. `git diff --check` passed.

The selected journal found the same generic PSBT length-prefixed boundary already indexed in tracked security evidence (`doc/security/codex-security-bitcoin/investigation-report.md` sections 9/10), independently reproduced it, and fixed it rather than issuing a duplicate claim. Before the fix, an empty `PSBT_OUT_TAP_INTERNAL_KEY` value caused the fixed-size parser to consume 32 bytes of a 36-byte later-map tail; a separate empty `PSBT_GLOBAL_UNSIGNED_TX` value caused the old transaction parser to consume ten following zero bytes. `UnserializeFromVector` now rejects values longer than the remaining stream, copies exactly the declared bytes into a bounded `SpanReader`, and requires that subreader to be exhausted. Three malformed-input regressions cover fixed-size cross-field consumption, global transaction cross-field consumption, and declared-length truncation. Source/test/journal commit `1b3e43702053a7cd4ede5b3d61f55c5b3730a72b` (`psbt: bound length-prefixed value deserialization`) is authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`. The UBSan build emitted only the existing `-O0` object-size-sanitizer warning. This confirms malformed-input boundary isolation and pre-rejection work/error locality, not a semantic PSBT acceptance bypass or quantified worst-case DoS. PR #35406 was used as a historical advisory seed: its 10,000-entry transaction queue cap did not cap each transaction's retained `send_statuses`, so a confirmed peer could keep a transaction alive while repeated retry/status records grew without that queue bound. - Confirmed finding: binary REST `/getutxos` applied its `MAX_GETUTXOS_OUTPOINTS=15` check after generic vector deserialization. The five-byte body `fe40420f00` declares one million outpoints; because the legacy wrapped stream includes the body length, the parser sees a six-byte stream and the old generic formatter reserves 138,888 `COutPoint` slots (`4,999,968` bytes) before the first missing element. `LIMITED_VECTOR(vOutPoints, MAX_GETUTXOS_OUTPOINTS)` rejects the CompactSize before allocation and maps the specific exception to `Error: max outpoints exceeded (max: 15)`; malformed/truncated entries retain `Parse error`.
- Source/test/journal commit: `d02675177e` (`rest: bound getutxos binary outpoint deserialization`), authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`. The pre-fix allocation probe, corrected wire fixture, source trace, and rejected P2P hypotheses are preserved in `agent-journal/resource-exhaustion-variants.md`.

- Confirmed finding: `CWallet::MarkReplaced` mutated `m_replaced_by_txid` and could mutate `m_state` before `WalletBatch::WriteTx` succeeded. Since bumpfee commits the replacement before calling this best-effort marker update, a failed write could return false while the live wallet reported the old transaction as replaced. The fix snapshots `m_state` and restores both fields on write failure; `wallet_tests/replaced_write_failure_preserves_state` injects a transaction-row SQLite failure and checks the marker and prior state.
- Source/test/journal finding commit: `c8d90a1f37` (`wallet: roll back replacement metadata on write failure`), authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- Validation: the old-source control exited 201 on the stale replacement-marker assertion; the repaired focused test passed; `wallet_tests,feebumper_tests,wallet_transaction_tests,walletdb_tests` passed 22 cases; the full `test_bitcoin` binary passed all 1,210 cases; the `test_bitcoin` rebuild and `git diff --check` passed. The trigger test exercises mock SQLite only and does not claim Berkeley DB or crash-restart coverage.
- The detailed evidence, history, source-to-sink trace, limitations, and next exclusions are in `agent-journal/local-reasoning-domain.md`. No relevant process remains running; preserved untracked artifacts remain excluded. The next cycle must perform a fresh gate and exact selector draw.

- Confirmed finding: `CWallet::SetAddressPreviouslySpent` changed `m_address_book[dest].previously_spent` before `DESTDATA/used` persistence. A failed write could therefore leave runtime and durable spent state inconsistent; the symmetric erase path had the reverse ordering risk.
- Fix: write the marker first, then publish in memory after a standalone success or active-transaction commit. Failed writes, aborts, and failed commits do not publish state.
- Source/test/journal commit: `600afa95995f5aaa50c23b6b6c2f940dc61674bb` (`wallet: publish spent state after successful write`), authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.
- Validation: final `test_bitcoin` build passed; the pre-fix source control exited `201` on seed `8901` with `!wallet.IsAddressPreviouslySpent(destination)` failing; the fixed focused test exited `0`; the fixed `wallet_tests` suite ran 18 cases with no errors; `git diff --check` passed.
- Limitation: the regression uses Mockable SQLite and does not separately inject `used=false` erase failure, Berkeley DB behavior, or power-loss/restart. The source path covers both boolean directions and active transaction callbacks. The caller still treats this secondary marker as best-effort; multi-input atomicity or fail-closed policy remains a distinct queued question.
- Next selector draw is pending. The next run must perform a fresh gate and choose a distinct catalog goal, rerolling only if the draw is the just-closed campaign without new evidence.

## Cycle 84 Verified Finding

- Finding: `CConnman::Start` returned `false` for `m_use_addrman_outgoing=true` plus nonempty `m_specified_outgoing` only after starting network thread handles. This violated the failed-start no-thread lifecycle invariant for direct public callers.
- Before evidence: the focused regression against the old source exited `201`; `!started` passed and `!threads_started` failed at `src/test/net_tests.cpp:391`.
- Repair: move the existing conflict check immediately after `Init`; add `ConnmanTestMsg::AnyThreadJoinablePublic()` and `net_tests/connman_start_rejects_conflicting_options_before_threads`.
- After evidence: the focused test exited `0`; full `net_tests` passed 34/34 cases and 150,899/150,899 assertions. The same build command and `/data`-backed `TMPDIR` were used; `git diff --check` passed.
- Cycle journal: `agent-journal/deterministic-simulation.md`; exact mechanism, reachability, history, limitations, and commands are recorded there. The commit is pending and must be authored as `Lőrinc <pap.lorinc@gmail.com>`.

- Finding: `TokenPipeEnd::TokenWrite()` returned `TS_ERR` for POSIX `EPIPE` after its last reader closed, despite `TS_EOS` being the documented end-of-stream status. `init.cpp` ignores `SIGPIPE`, and `SignalInterrupt` propagates the nonzero write result, making the distinction observable. `c28bbc9580` maps EPIPE to TS_EOS and adds a POSIX regression for normal transfer and both EOF directions.
- Verification: the parent implementation failed the new regression at `-1 != -2` with exit 201; the fixed normal focused test passed 6/6 assertions, full `util_tests` passed 79 cases and 3,991 assertions, the focused TSan/UBSan run passed 6/6, and the libc++/MSan direct probe exited 0 without diagnostics. Direct `scan-build-19` and GCC `-fanalyzer` on `tokenpipe.cpp` emitted no report. The full MSan daemon runtime stopped at uninstrumented system libstdc++ static initialization; Valgrind and other queued analyzers were unavailable.
